Well, I... I really don't want to sound like I'm whining, because I know I have so much to be thankful for. But emotionally, I guess... it's just been a bit hard lately. 

Most mornings, I... well, I wake up and there's just this... this heavy sadness right here in my chest. It's almost like... like I've already failed the day before I even get out of bed. I try so hard to just put a smile on and push through it, because I don't want to burden anyone else with it, you know? But it just sits there. And then by the time evening rolls around, I'm just so worn out from trying to keep it together... that's usually when those bad thoughts slip in. 

I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't let myself feel that way. Is it... is it normal to feel that kind of heaviness in the mornings? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it.

Well, I... I suppose so, yes. I hope that doesn't make me sound lazy or ungrateful for the free time I have. I really do try to stay active, I promise.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I really do try to keep my pace up, I promise. I don't want to be a burden or slow anyone down. But... my husband, he's been so patient with me, he did mention recently that I move like everything takes extra effort now. I hope it doesn't make me sound lazy. I really am trying to push through it. 

And... well, even when I go to the market, I've noticed I just stand there for too long before picking up what I came for. I just sort of stare at the shelves and can't seem to make my hands work right away. I always try to snap out of it quickly so I'm not blocking the aisle or getting in anyone's way. 

I know I should be moving faster, and I feel awful about dragging my feet. Is... is it normal to get a little sluggish like that when you're feeling down? I hope I'm explaining it okay for you.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, I... well, I hate to admit it, because I really do try to keep up with everything around the house, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around all day letting things go. But... yes, I suppose I have been feeling quite tired lately. 

Even after a full night in bed, I wake up and my body just feels so... worn out. It’s strange, because I haven't done anything to deserve being that tired. Just doing the normal things, like carrying a basket of laundry down the hall or... or just making a bit of breakfast... it seems to take so much more out of me than it really should. 

I try to push through it so my husband doesn't have to pick up my slack, but it's been difficult. I feel so silly even mentioning it, because it's not like I'm out doing hard labor or anything. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy. Is it... does that sound normal to you, getting so worn out over such little things?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I know we just talked about the watercolors and the walking... and I really don't want to sound like a broken record, or like I'm just complaining. I promise I try to find the bright side of things.

I leave my paints sitting right there on the table, and I can't seem to make myself care about picking them up. Oh, um... well, I really try to put on a brave face, especially for my husband. I don't want to bring him down with me, you know? I want to be pleasant to be around. But... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't make me sound terrible... yes.

It's there before I even get out of bed, like I've already failed the day before it even starts. And nothing bad has happened, which makes me feel so guilty for feeling that way. I have a roof over my head and a wonderful husband, I shouldn't be so gloomy. \n\nI try my hardest to shake it off, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just wallowing in it. I think my appetite has been pretty much the same as always, and my clothes all seem to fit just fine. I try to be good and finish what's on my plate so nothing goes to waste. \n\nI hope that's okay? I know I haven't been very helpful with some of the other things, but I really haven't noticed any weight loss or anything. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, my... well, I always try to do the right thing, I promise I do. I try to be good to everyone and just do my part. 

But... sometimes, and I feel just terrible even admitting this to you out loud... sometimes when I'm with my aunt, I get a little tired. Or maybe just a tiny bit annoyed. I love her dearly, I really do, and I don't want you to think I don't! 

But when I get tired like that... oh, I just start thinking I'm a bad woman. I get so upset with myself, and I feel this awful guilt, like I'm bringing shame on my whole family for not being more grateful and patient with her. I shouldn't have thoughts like that, I know. I try so hard to push them away and just count my blessings. 

I hope you don't think poorly of me for saying all that... I really am trying to be better. Did I answer your question okay?

And I had a little dance class I went to, and I'd go walking with some of the ladies from the neighborhood. They're such lovely women. But recently, I just... I leave my paints sitting right there on the table, and I can't seem to make myself care about picking them up. And I've been making excuses to the ladies about the walks. \n\nI know I should just force myself to go and do these things, and I feel terrible for letting my hobbies slide. It's just... the joy kind of went out of it, if that makes sense? I hope you don't think I'm just giving up. I really am trying to be better about it. Is it... is it okay that I've been skipping those things?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you've been moving more slowly than usual or that it's harder to get your body to do what you want, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really do try to keep my pace up, I promise. I don't want to be a burden or slow anyone down. But... my husband, he's been so patient with me, he did mention recently that I move like everything takes extra effort now. I hope it doesn't make me sound lazy. I really am trying to push through it. \n\nAnd... well, even when I go to the market, I've noticed I just stand there for too long before picking up what I came for. I just sort of stare at the shelves and can't seem to make my hands work right away. I always try to snap out of it quickly so I'm not blocking the aisle or getting in anyone's way. \n\nI know I should be moving faster, and I feel awful about dragging my feet. Is... is it normal to get a little sluggish like that when you're feeling down? I hope I'm explaining it okay for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired or that you have less energy than usual, even when you haven't done anything physically exhausting?\n\nPatient: Oh, I... well, I hate to admit it, because I really do try to keep up with everything around the house, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around all day letting things go. But... yes, I suppose I have been feeling quite tired lately. \n\nEven after a full night in bed, I wake up and my body just feels so... worn out. It’s strange, because I haven't done anything to deserve being that tired. Just doing the normal things, like carrying a basket of laundry down the hall or... or just making a bit of breakfast... it seems to take so much more out of me than it really should. \n\nI try to push through it so my husband doesn't have to pick up my slack, but it's been difficult. I feel so silly even mentioning it, because it's not like I'm out doing hard labor or anything. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy. Is it... does that sound normal to you, getting so worn out over such little things?\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when even things you used to really enjoy didn't bring you any happiness at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I know we just talked about the watercolors and the walking... and I really don't want to sound like a broken record, or like I'm just complaining. I promise I try to find the bright side of things. \n\nBut... I suppose, even when I do force myself to sit down with my paints, or when my husband brings me my favorite tea... it just doesn't give me that little lift it used to. I smile and thank him, of course, because he's so sweet to try, and I don't want to seem ungrateful. But inside, it just feels a bit... flat, I guess? \n\nI really am trying to appreciate the nice things, I promise. I know I have a good life and shouldn't be feeling this way. I hope I'm not frustrating you by saying all this... I'm answering your questions right, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been days when you feel down or sad most of the time, from morning until night, even when nothing particularly bad has happened?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really try to put on a brave face, especially for my husband. I don't want to bring him down with me, you know? I want to be pleasant to be around. But... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't make me sound terrible... yes. \n\nMost mornings, I wake up and there's just this... this heavy sadness sitting right in my chest. It's there before I even get out of bed, like I've already failed the day before it even starts. And nothing bad has happened, which makes me feel so guilty for feeling that way. I have a roof over my head and a wonderful husband, I shouldn't be so gloomy. \n\nI try my hardest to shake it off, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just wallowing in it. But it just sort of hangs around all day, no matter how hard I try to smile through it. Is... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, like not feeling hungry as often or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really try to make sure I'm eating enough, I promise. My husband cooks such lovely meals, and I always sit down with him at the table because I don't want to hurt his feelings or seem ungrateful. \n\nBut... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I think my appetite has been pretty much the same as always, and my clothes all seem to fit just fine. I try to be good and finish what's on my plate so nothing goes to waste. \n\nI hope that's okay? I know I haven't been very helpful with some of the other things, but I really haven't noticed any weight loss or anything. Did I answer that right for you?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, maybe even blaming yourself for things that aren't your fault, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I always try to do the right thing, I promise I do. I try to be good to everyone and just do my part. \n\nBut... sometimes, and I feel just terrible even admitting this to you out loud... sometimes when I'm with my aunt, I get a little tired. Or maybe just a tiny bit annoyed. I love her dearly, I really do, and I don't want you to think I don't! \n\nBut when I get tired like that... oh, I just start thinking I'm a bad woman. I get so upset with myself, and I feel this awful guilt, like I'm bringing shame on my whole family for not being more grateful and patient with her. I shouldn't have thoughts like that, I know. I try so hard to push them away and just count my blessings. \n\nI hope you don't think poorly of me for saying all that... I really am trying to be better. Did I answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times recently when even spending time with people you care about, or doing things you used to enjoy, just felt like going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really do try to stay active, I promise. I used to just love my watercolors. I'd set them all out and paint the flowers in the garden. And my dance class, too! I just sort of leave my painting things sitting there on the table. And when it's time for class or a walk, I just... I can't seem to make myself care about it anymore. It feels like such a chore, even though I know I should be enjoying it. I still go sometimes, because I don't want to let the ladies down, but it's just not the same. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I should be making more of an effort. I really will try harder to get back into it. Is that the sort of thing you meant? I hope I'm not being too much trouble with my answers.\n\nDoctor: Have there been days when you felt like nothing — not even the things you usually care about — could bring you any pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, I really do try to find the good in every day, Doctor. I make a point to smile and be pleasant, especially when my husband is around, because I don't want to bring him down or seem like a grump. \n\nBut... oh, I suppose there are days where things just feel a little... flat? I don't want to say nothing brings me pleasure, because that sounds so ungrateful when I have such a nice home and a good family. I always make sure to say thank you when my husband makes dinner or when my aunt calls. \n\nI just... sometimes it feels like I'm smiling on the outside, but on the inside, I'm just sort of quiet. I still care about everything, of course I do! I just don't always feel that spark of joy I know I'm supposed to feel. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound awful. I really am trying my best to stay positive. Did that make sense, Doctor? I have a roof over my head and a wonderful husband, I shouldn't be so gloomy. \n\nI try my hardest to shake it off, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just wallowing in it. But it just sort of hangs around all day, no matter how hard I try to smile through it. Is... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, like not feeling hungry as often or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really try to make sure I'm eating enough, I promise. My husband cooks such lovely meals, and I always sit down with him at the table because I don't want to hurt his feelings or seem ungrateful. \n\nBut... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I think my appetite has been pretty much the same as always, and my clothes all seem to fit just fine. I try to be good and finish what's on my plate so nothing goes to waste. \n\nI hope that's okay? I know I haven't been very helpful with some of the other things, but I really haven't noticed any weight loss or anything. Did I answer that right for you?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, maybe even blaming yourself for things that aren't your fault, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I always try to do the right thing, I promise I do. I try to be good to everyone and just do my part. \n\nBut... sometimes, and I feel just terrible even admitting this to you out loud... sometimes when I'm with my aunt, I get a little tired. Or maybe just a tiny bit annoyed. I love her dearly, I really do, and I don't want you to think I don't! \n\nBut when I get tired like that... oh, I just start thinking I'm a bad woman. I get so upset with myself, and I feel this awful guilt, like I'm bringing shame on my whole family for not being more grateful and patient with her. I shouldn't have thoughts like that, I know. I try so hard to push them away and just count my blessings. \n\nI hope you don't think poorly of me for saying all that... I really am trying to be better. Did I answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times recently when even spending time with people you care about, or doing things you used to enjoy, just felt like going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really do try to stay active, I promise. I used to just love my watercolors. I'd set them all out and paint the flowers in the garden. And my dance class, too! I just sort of leave my painting things sitting there on the table. And when it's time for class or a walk, I just... I can't seem to make myself care about it anymore. It feels like such a chore, even though I know I should be enjoying it. I still go sometimes, because I don't want to let the ladies down, but it's just not the same. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I should be making more of an effort. I really will try harder to get back into it. Is that the sort of thing you meant? I don't want to say nothing brings me pleasure, because that sounds so ungrateful when I have such a nice home and a good family. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh my... Doctor, I am so sorry. I must be doing a dreadful job of explaining myself if you have to keep asking me. Please forgive me, I really don't mean to be difficult or make your job harder. 

I just... I'm not sure how else to say it without sounding like a completely lazy person. It's just like I was saying about my watercolors, and the dance class, and walking with the ladies. I know I keep bringing those up, but they used to be my favorite things in the whole world. And now... I just look at my painting things sitting right there on the table, and I can't even pick up a brush. I just can't make myself care about going out and doing any of it anymore, even though I know I really should. 

I know how awful and ungrateful that sounds, and I promise I'm trying my hardest to push past it. Am I... did I answer it wrong before? Please tell me if there's a different way I should be explaining things. I just want to make sure I'm being a good patient for you.

I hope I'm explaining it okay for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired or that you have less energy than usual, even when you haven't done anything physically exhausting?\n\nPatient: Oh, I... well, I hate to admit it, because I really do try to keep up with everything around the house, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around all day letting things go. But... yes, I suppose I have been feeling quite tired lately. \n\nEven after a full night in bed, I wake up and my body just feels so... worn out. It’s strange, because I haven't done anything to deserve being that tired. Just doing the normal things, like carrying a basket of laundry down the hall or... or just making a bit of breakfast... it seems to take so much more out of me than it really should. \n\nI try to push through it so my husband doesn't have to pick up my slack, but it's been difficult. I feel so silly even mentioning it, because it's not like I'm out doing hard labor or anything. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy. Is it... does that sound normal to you, getting so worn out over such little things?\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when even things you used to really enjoy didn't bring you any happiness at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I know we just talked about the watercolors and the walking... and I really don't want to sound like a broken record, or like I'm just complaining. I promise I try to find the bright side of things. \n\nBut... Did I answer that right for you?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, maybe even blaming yourself for things that aren't your fault, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I always try to do the right thing, I promise I do. Did I answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times recently when even spending time with people you care about, or doing things you used to enjoy, just felt like going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really do try to stay active, I promise. I feel so silly even mentioning it, because it's not like I'm out doing hard labor or anything. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy. I promise I try to find the bright side of things. \n\nBut... I smile and thank him, of course, because he's so sweet to try, and I don't want to seem ungrateful. But inside, it just feels a bit... flat, I guess? \n\nI really am trying to appreciate the nice things, I promise. I know I have a good life and shouldn't be feeling this way. I hope I'm not frustrating you by saying all this... I'm answering your questions right, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been days when you feel down or sad most of the time, from morning until night, even when nothing particularly bad has happened?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really try to put on a brave face, especially for my husband. I don't want to bring him down with me, you know? I want to be pleasant to be around. But... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't make me sound terrible... yes. \n\nMost mornings, I wake up and there's just this... this heavy sadness sitting right in my chest. But... my husband, he's been so patient with me, he did mention recently that I move like everything takes extra effort now. I hope it doesn't make me sound lazy. I really am trying to push through it. \n\nAnd... well, even when I go to the market, I've noticed I just stand there for too long before picking up what I came for. I just sort of stare at the shelves and can't seem to make my hands work right away. I always try to snap out of it quickly so I'm not blocking the aisle or getting in anyone's way. \n\nI know I should be moving faster, and I feel awful about dragging my feet. Is... is it normal to get a little sluggish like that when you're feeling down? I hope I'm explaining it okay for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired or that you have less energy than usual, even when you haven't done anything physically exhausting?\n\nPatient: Oh, I... well, I hate to admit it, because I really do try to keep up with everything around the house, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around all day letting things go. But... yes, I suppose I have been feeling quite tired lately. \n\nEven after a full night in bed, I wake up and my body just feels so... worn out. It’s strange, because I haven't done anything to deserve being that tired. Just doing the normal things, like carrying a basket of laundry down the hall or... or just making a bit of breakfast... it seems to take so much more out of me than it really should. \n\nI try to push through it so my husband doesn't have to pick up my slack, but it's been difficult. I feel so silly even mentioning it, because it's not like I'm out doing hard labor or anything. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy. Is it... does that sound normal to you, getting so worn out over such little things?\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when even things you used to really enjoy didn't bring you any happiness at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I know we just talked about the watercolors and the walking... and I really don't want to sound like a broken record, or like I'm just complaining. I promise I try to find the bright side of things. \n\nBut... I suppose, even when I do force myself to sit down with my paints, or when my husband brings me my favorite tea... it just doesn't give me that little lift it used to. I smile and thank him, of course, because he's so sweet to try, and I don't want to seem ungrateful. But inside, it just feels a bit... flat, I guess? \n\nI really am trying to appreciate the nice things, I promise. I know I have a good life and shouldn't be feeling this way. I hope I'm not frustrating you by saying all this... I'm answering your questions right, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been days when you feel down or sad most of the time, from morning until night, even when nothing particularly bad has happened?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really try to put on a brave face, especially for my husband. I don't want to bring him down with me, you know? I want to be pleasant to be around. But... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't make me sound terrible... yes. \n\nMost mornings, I wake up and there's just this... this heavy sadness sitting right in my chest. It's there before I even get out of bed, like I've already failed the day before it even starts. And nothing bad has happened, which makes me feel so guilty for feeling that way. I have a roof over my head and a wonderful husband, I shouldn't be so gloomy. \n\nI try my hardest to shake it off, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just wallowing in it. But it just sort of hangs around all day, no matter how hard I try to smile through it. Is... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, like not feeling hungry as often or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really try to make sure I'm eating enough, I promise. My husband cooks such lovely meals, and I always sit down with him at the table because I don't want to hurt his feelings or seem ungrateful. \n\nBut... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I think my appetite has been pretty much the same as always, and my clothes all seem to fit just fine. I try to be good and finish what's on my plate so nothing goes to waste. \n\nI hope that's okay? I know I haven't been very helpful with some of the other things, but I really haven't noticed any weight loss or anything. Did I answer that right for you?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, maybe even blaming yourself for things that aren't your fault, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I always try to do the right thing, I promise I do. I try to be good to everyone and just do my part. \n\nBut... sometimes, and I feel just terrible even admitting this to you out loud... sometimes when I'm with my aunt, I get a little tired. Or maybe just a tiny bit annoyed. I love her dearly, I really do, and I don't want you to think I don't! \n\nBut when I get tired like that... oh, I just start thinking I'm a bad woman. I get so upset with myself, and I feel this awful guilt, like I'm bringing shame on my whole family for not being more grateful and patient with her. I shouldn't have thoughts like that, I know. I try so hard to push them away and just count my blessings. \n\nI hope you don't think poorly of me for saying all that... I really am trying to be better. Did I answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times recently when even spending time with people you care about, or doing things you used to enjoy, just felt like going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really do try to stay active, I promise. I know how important it is to keep busy at my age, so I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. \n\nBut... oh, it's a little embarrassing to admit. I used to just love my watercolors. I'd set them all out and paint the flowers in the garden. And my dance class, too! Plus walking with the ladies from the neighborhood... we always had such a nice time. \n\nLately, though... I hope this doesn't sound lazy of me... I just sort of leave my painting things sitting there on the table. And when it's time for class or a walk, I just... I can't seem to make myself care about it anymore. It feels like such a chore, even though I know I should be enjoying it. I still go sometimes, because I don't want to let the ladies down, but it's just not the same. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I should be making more of an effort. I really will try harder to get back into it. Is that the sort of thing you meant? I hope I'm not being too much trouble with my answers.\n\nDoctor: Have there been days when you felt like nothing — not even the things you usually care about — could bring you any pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, I really do try to find the good in every day, Doctor. I make a point to smile and be pleasant, especially when my husband is around, because I don't want to bring him down or seem like a grump. \n\nBut... oh, I suppose there are days where things just feel a little... flat? I don't want to say nothing brings me pleasure, because that sounds so ungrateful when I have such a nice home and a good family. I always make sure to say thank you when my husband makes dinner or when my aunt calls. \n\nI just... sometimes it feels like I'm smiling on the outside, but on the inside, I'm just sort of quiet. I still care about everything, of course I do! I just don't always feel that spark of joy I know I'm supposed to feel. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound awful. I really am trying my best to stay positive. Did that make sense, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times recently when absolutely nothing — not even things you usually care about or enjoy — seemed to bring you any sense of pleasure or satisfaction at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, Doctor... I know we just touched on this a moment ago, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much. I really do want to give you a good answer. \n\nIt's just like I was saying about my watercolors and the dance class... and my walks with the ladies. I used to look forward to all of that so much. And when it's time to go out, I just... I can't seem to make myself care about it. It just doesn't bring me that happy feeling it used to, no matter how hard I try to force it.\n\nI'm so sorry if I'm not explaining it well, or if it's not exactly what you were asking. I really am trying to be helpful. But ever since then, it's just been... day after day of feeling this way.
